    "oldString": "then jump to 6/8 at $\\alpha{=}0.50$ across all 3~fp32 seeds.",
    "newString": "then jump to 6/8 at $\\alpha{=}0.50$ across all 3~fp32 seeds, indicating robust detection with no significant seed variance at this critical transition.",
    "originalFile": null,
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 18,
        "oldLines": 7,
        "newStart": 18,
        "newLines": 7,
        "lines": [
          " Diversity collapse occurs at every contamination level: distinct-1 declines by $14.1\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.0$ (fine-tuning drift) and by $40.3{\\pm}0.3\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.25$ (3-seed mean).",
          " Despite this ubiquitous degradation, Granger detectability is sharply regime-dependent.",
          " The onset we report reflects PermG's statistical power curve crossing the detection threshold at a critical contamination level, not the emergence of a new physical signal; this interpretation is confirmed by sub-threshold $p$-values at $\\alpha{=}0.45$ (\\S\\ref{sec:theory}).",
          "-Permutation Granger (PermG) tests find 0/8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs FDR-significant from $\\alpha{=}0.00$ through $\\alpha{=}0.45$, then jump to 6/8 at $\\alpha{=}0.50$ across all 3~fp32 seeds.",
          "+Permutation Granger (PermG) tests find 0/8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs FDR-significant from $\\alpha{=}0.00$ through $\\alpha{=}0.45$, then jump to 6/8 at $\\alpha{=}0.50$ across all 3~fp32 seeds, indicating robust detection with no significant seed variance at this critical transition.",
          " This transition is striking because cumulative synthetic fraction barely changes across the boundary: $C = 1{-}(1{-}\\alpha)^{10}$ yields $99.75\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.45$ versus $99.90\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.50$, a difference of 0.15 percentage points, yet detection jumps from null to reliable.",
          " Sigmoid regression on the 13-point $T{=}10$ grid yields $\\alpha_c = 0.483 \\pm 0.006$ ($R^2 = 0.969$); dense $\\alpha$ sampling (17~values) constrains the onset steepness to $k{=}15.7$ (95\\% CI $[9.4, 28.2]$), a steep but finite-width sigmoid (Figure~\\ref{fig:regime_map}).",
          " This single-$T$ estimate is consistent with the cross-$T$ validated model (\\S\\ref{sec:theory}), which predicts how the detection boundary shifts with observation length ($\\gamma{=}2.08$)."
        ]
      }
    ],

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01SVE3PjFx5HNG5Aspgq4q1P`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Granger precedence is not causation.}
4	Our claim is scoped to temporal predictive improvement~\cite{granger1969investigating,shojaie2022granger}; bivariate tests cannot exclude confounders, and our cluster-adjusted FPR CI ($[0\%, 41\%]$; \S\ref{sec:limitations}) means true Type~I error may be non-negligible.
5	
6	\paragraph{Non-confirming cells.}
7	Five of twenty phase-diagram cells show $0/8$ despite lying above the theoretical detection boundary: four are sync-dip cells ($\alpha{=}0.60$, $T{=}10$--$20$) and one is a boundary cell ($T{=}10$, $\alpha{=}0.45$).
8	The sync-dip dissolves by $T{=}25$ ($5/8$), confirming it is transient; its mechanism remains open---one hypothesis is synchronization between the replacement ratio and the model's internal mode dynamics.
9	Excluding these five cells, the remaining 15/15 match theory (LOO-CV MAE${=}0.037$).
10	
11	\paragraph{Metric-specific ceiling.}
12	Observed detection counts never exceed $6/8$: the 2/8 non-detected pairs involve MAUVE, whose floor saturation ($\mu{=}0.029$) eliminates the temporal gradient Granger precedence requires (\S\ref{sec:t20}); practitioners should prefer monotonically degrading metrics (e.g., distinct-$n$).
13	
14	\paragraph{Cross-architecture failure modes.}
15	Five of fifteen conditions do not confirm forward detection (\S\ref{sec:generalization}): precision sensitivity (GPT-2 bf16: 0/8 vs fp32 6/8), bidirectional confounding (GPT-2 s43: reverse 6/8), seed dependence (Pythia-1.4B: s42 2/8 vs s43 6/8), and architecture-specific decoupling (SmolLM3-3B entropy 0/4, violating mode amplification; Proposition~\ref{thm:entropy_leads}).
16	Phase-diagram replication at 1.4B confirms qualitatively preserved detection despite count attenuation (4--5/8; Appendix~\ref{app:scale_14b}).
17	
18	\paragraph{Entropy-diversity coupling.}
19	The entropy-diversity lag is structural: distinct-$n$ requires token mass to cross threshold~$\varepsilon$ ($\gamma$-margin; Proposition~\ref{prop:div_lag}), producing the ${\geq}1$-generation lag in 14/15 experiments; PermG's directional asymmetry (6/8 forward, 0/8 reverse) argues against a sensitivity artifact.
20	
21	\paragraph{Domain-dependent directionality.}
22	WikiText yields exclusively forward PermG significance; C4 produces reverse 7/8 at $\alpha{=}0.50$ (Appendix~\ref{app:extended_discussion}), indicating domain-dependent confounding.
23	
24	\paragraph{Practical implications.}
25	Longer $T$ lowers $\alpha_c$ sub-linearly (\S\ref{sec:theory}); PermG requires fp32 (bf16: 0/8 vs 6/8 in GPT-2).
26	Entropy is the optimal canary (14/15 cascade); triggered cessation reduces peak perplexity by 58\% (\S\ref{sec:intervention}), bridging the detection-to-action gap that prior work leaves open.
27	
```

</details>

practitioners should prefer monotonically degrading metrics (e.g., distinct-$n$).\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-architecture failure modes.}\nFive of fifteen conditions do not confirm forward detection (\\S\\ref{sec:generalization}): precision sensitivity (GPT-2 bf16: 0/8 vs fp32 6/8), bidirectional confounding (GPT-2 s43: reverse 6/8), seed dependence (Pythia-1.4B: s42 2/8 vs s43 6/8), and architecture-specific decoupling (SmolLM3-3B entropy 0/4, violating mode amplification; Proposition~\\ref{thm:entropy_leads}).\nPhase-diagram replication at 1.4B confirms qualitatively preserved detection despite count attenuation (4--5/8; Appendix~\\ref{app:scale_14b}).\n\n\\paragraph{Entropy-diversity coupling.}\nThe entropy-diversity lag is structural: distinct-$n$ requires token mass to cross threshold~$\\varepsilon$ ($\\gamma$-margin; Proposition~\\ref{prop:div_lag}), producing the ${\\geq}1$-generation lag in 14/15 experiments; PermG's directional asymmetry (6/8 forward, 0/8 reverse) argues against a sensitivity artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Domain-dependent directionality.}\nWikiText yields exclusively forward PermG significance; C4 produces reverse 7/8 at $\\alpha{=}0.50$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}), indicating domain-dependent confounding.\n\n\\paragraph{Practical implications.}\nLonger $T$ lowers $\\alpha_c$ sub-linearly (\\S\\ref{sec:theory}); PermG requires fp32 (bf16: 0/8 vs 6/8 in GPT-2).\nEntropy is the optimal canary (14/15 cascade); triggered cessation reduces peak perplexity by 58\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:intervention}), bridging the detection-to-action gap that prior work leaves open.\n",

    "newString": "\\paragraph{Entropy-diversity coupling.}\nThe entropy-diversity lag is structural: distinct-$n$ requires token mass to cross threshold~$\\varepsilon$ ($\\gamma$-margin; Proposition~\\ref{prop:div_lag}), producing the ${\\geq}1$-generation lag in 14/15 experiments; PermG's directional asymmetry (6/8 forward, 0/8 reverse) argues against a sensitivity artifact.\nHowever, directionality is domain-dependent: WikiText yields exclusively forward significance, while C4 produces reverse 7/8 at $\\alpha{=}0.50$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Granger precedence is not causation.}\nOur claim is scoped to temporal predictive improvement~\\cite{granger1969investigating,shojaie2022granger}; bivariate tests cannot exclude confounders, and our cluster-adjusted FPR CI ($[0\\%, 41\\%]$; \\S\\ref{sec:limitations}) means true Type~I error may be non-negligible.\n\n\\paragraph{Non-confirming cells.}\nFive of twenty phase-diagram cells show $0/8$ despite lying above the theoretical detection boundary: four are sync-dip cells ($\\alpha{=}0.60$, $T{=}10$--$20$) and one is a boundary cell ($T{=}10$, $\\alpha{=}0.45$).\nThe sync-dip dissolves by $T{=}25$ ($5/8$), confirming it is transient; its mechanism remains open---one hypothesis is synchronization between the replacement ratio and the model's internal mode dynamics.\nExcluding these five cells, the remaining 15/15 match theory (LOO-CV MAE${=}0.037$).\n\n\\paragraph{Metric-specific ceiling.}\nObserved detection counts never exceed $6/8$: the 2/8 non-detected pairs involve MAUVE, whose floor saturation ($\\mu{=}0.029$) eliminates the temporal gradient Granger precedence requires (\\S\\ref{sec:t20}); practitioners should prefer monotonically degrading metrics (e.g., distinct-$n$).\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-architecture failure modes.}\nFive of fifteen conditions do not confirm forward detection (\\S\\ref{sec:generalization}): precision sensitivity (GPT-2 bf16: 0/8 vs fp32 6/8), bidirectional confounding (GPT-2 s43: reverse 6/8), seed dependence (Pythia-1.4B: s42 2/8 vs s43 6/8), and architecture-specific decoupling (SmolLM3-3B entropy 0/4, violating mode amplification; Proposition~\\ref{thm:entropy_leads}).\nPhase-diagram replication at 1.4B confirms qualitatively preserved detection despite count attenuation (4--5/8; Appendix~\\ref{app:scale_14b}).\n\n\\paragraph{Entropy-diversity coupling.}\nThe entropy-diversity lag is structural: distinct-$n$ requires token mass to cross threshold~$\\varepsilon$ ($\\gamma$-margin; Proposition~\\ref{prop:div_lag}), producing the ${\\geq}1$-generation lag in 14/15 experiments; PermG's directional asymmetry (6/8 forward, 0/8 reverse) argues against a sensitivity artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Domain-dependent directionality.}\nWikiText yields exclusively forward PermG significance; C4 produces reverse 7/8 at $\\alpha{=}0.50$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}), indicating domain-dependent confounding.\n\n\\paragraph{Practical implications.}\nLonger $T$ lowers $\\alpha_c$ sub-linearly (\\S\\ref{sec:theory}); PermG requires fp32 (bf16: 0/8 vs 6/8 in GPT-2).\nTriggered cessation on entropy (optimal canary; 14/15 cascade) reduces peak perplexity by 58\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:intervention}), bridging detection to action.\n",

          " Diversity collapse occurs at every contamination level: distinct-1 declines by $14.1\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.0$ (fine-tuning drift) and by $40.3{\\pm}0.3\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.25$ (3-seed mean).",
          " Despite this ubiquitous degradation, Granger detectability is sharply regime-dependent.",
          " The onset we report reflects PermG's statistical power curve crossing the detection threshold at a critical contamination level, not the emergence of a new physical signal; this interpretation is confirmed by sub-threshold $p$-values at $\\alpha{=}0.45$ (\\S\\ref{sec:theory}).",
          "-Permutation Granger (PermG) tests find 0/8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs FDR-significant from $\\alpha{=}0.00$ through $\\alpha{=}0.45$, then jump to 6/8 at $\\alpha{=}0.50$ across all 3~fp32 seeds, indicating robust detection with no significant seed variance at this critical transition.",
          "+Permutation Granger (PermG) tests find 0/8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs FDR-significant from $\\alpha{=}0.00$ through $\\alpha{=}0.45$, then jump to 6/8 at $\\alpha{=}0.50$ uniformly across all 3~fp32 seeds.",
          " This transition is striking because cumulative synthetic fraction barely changes across the boundary: $C = 1{-}(1{-}\\alpha)^{10}$ yields $99.75\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.45$ versus $99.90\\%$ at $\\alpha{=}0.50$, a difference of 0.15 percentage points, yet detection jumps from null to reliable.",
          " Sigmoid regression on the 13-point $T{=}10$ grid yields $\\alpha_c = 0.483 \\pm 0.006$ ($R^2 = 0.969$); dense $\\alpha$ sampling (17~values) constrains the onset steepness to $k{=}15.7$ (95\\% CI $[9.4, 28.2]$), a steep but finite-width sigmoid (Figure~\\ref{fig:regime_map}).",
          " This single-$T$ estimate is consistent with the cross-$T$ validated model (\\S\\ref{sec:theory}), which predicts how the detection boundary shifts with observation length ($\\gamma{=}2.08$)."

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Granger precedence is not causation.}
4	Our claim is scoped to temporal predictive improvement~\cite{granger1969investigating,shojaie2022granger}; bivariate tests cannot exclude confounders, and our cluster-adjusted FPR CI ($[0\%, 41\%]$; \S\ref{sec:limitations}) means true Type~I error may be non-negligible.
5	
6	\paragraph{Non-confirming cells.}
7	Five of twenty phase-diagram cells show $0/8$ despite lying above the theoretical detection boundary: four are sync-dip cells ($\alpha{=}0.60$, $T{=}10$--$20$) and one is a boundary cell ($T{=}10$, $\alpha{=}0.45$).
8	The sync-dip dissolves by $T{=}25$ ($5/8$), confirming it is transient; its mechanism remains open---one hypothesis is synchronization between the replacement ratio and the model's internal mode dynamics.
9	Excluding these five cells, the remaining 15/15 match theory (LOO-CV MAE${=}0.037$).
10	
11	\paragraph{Metric-specific ceiling.}
12	Observed detection counts never exceed $6/8$: the 2/8 non-detected pairs involve MAUVE, whose floor saturation ($\mu{=}0.029$) eliminates the temporal gradient Granger precedence requires (\S\ref{sec:t20}); practitioners should prefer monotonically degrading metrics (e.g., distinct-$n$).
13	
14	\paragraph{Cross-architecture failure modes.}
15	Five of fifteen conditions do not confirm forward detection (\S\ref{sec:generalization}): precision sensitivity (GPT-2 bf16: 0/8 vs fp32 6/8), bidirectional confounding (GPT-2 s43: reverse 6/8), seed dependence (Pythia-1.4B: s42 2/8 vs s43 6/8), and architecture-specific decoupling (SmolLM3-3B entropy 0/4, violating mode amplification; Proposition~\ref{thm:entropy_leads}).
16	Phase-diagram replication at 1.4B confirms qualitatively preserved detection despite count attenuation (4--5/8; Appendix~\ref{app:scale_14b}).
17	
18	\paragraph{Entropy-diversity coupling.}
19	The entropy-diversity lag is structural: distinct-$n$ requires token mass to cross threshold~$\varepsilon$ ($\gamma$-margin; Proposition~\ref{prop:div_lag}), producing the ${\geq}1$-generation lag in 14/15 experiments; PermG's directional asymmetry (6/8 forward, 0/8 reverse) argues against a sensitivity artifact.
20	However, directionality is domain-dependent: WikiText yields exclusively forward significance, while C4 produces reverse 7/8 at $\alpha{=}0.50$ (Appendix~\ref{app:extended_discussion}).
21	
22	\paragraph{Practical implications.}
23	Longer $T$ lowers $\alpha_c$ sub-linearly (\S\ref{sec:theory}); PermG requires fp32 (bf16: 0/8 vs 6/8 in GPT-2).
24	Triggered cessation on entropy (optimal canary; 14/15 cascade) reduces peak perplexity by 58\% (\S\ref{sec:intervention}), bridging detection to action.
25	
```

practitioners should prefer monotonically degrading metrics (e.g., distinct-$n$).\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-architecture failure modes.}\nFive of fifteen conditions do not confirm forward detection (\\S\\ref{sec:generalization}): precision sensitivity (GPT-2 bf16: 0/8 vs fp32 6/8), bidirectional confounding (GPT-2 s43: reverse 6/8), seed dependence (Pythia-1.4B: s42 2/8 vs s43 6/8), and architecture-specific decoupling (SmolLM3-3B entropy 0/4, violating mode amplification; Proposition~\\ref{thm:entropy_leads}).\nPhase-diagram replication at 1.4B confirms qualitatively preserved detection despite count attenuation (4--5/8; Appendix~\\ref{app:scale_14b}).\n\n\\paragraph{Entropy-diversity coupling.}\nThe entropy-diversity lag is structural: distinct-$n$ requires token mass to cross threshold~$\\varepsilon$ ($\\gamma$-margin; Proposition~\\ref{prop:div_lag}), producing the ${\\geq}1$-generation lag in 14/15 experiments; PermG's directional asymmetry (6/8 forward, 0/8 reverse) argues against a sensitivity artifact.\nHowever, directionality is domain-dependent: WikiText yields exclusively forward significance, while C4 produces reverse 7/8 at $\\alpha{=}0.50$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}).\n\n\\paragraph{Practical implications.}\nLonger $T$ lowers $\\alpha_c$ sub-linearly (\\S\\ref{sec:theory}); PermG requires fp32 (bf16: 0/8 vs 6/8 in GPT-2).\nTriggered cessation on entropy (optimal canary; 14/15 cascade) reduces peak perplexity by 58\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:intervention}), bridging detection to action.\n",
